Errors are captured in self._stdin_error and\n        # surfaced via _log.warning from wait()/terminate().\n        self._stdin_error: Optional[BaseException] = None\n        self._write_prompt_to_stdin(self._prompt_bytes)\n\n        if self._on_spawn is not None:\n            self._on_spawn(self._process.pid)\n\n        _log.debug(\n            \"spawn pid=%d cmd=%s prompt_bytes=%d\",\n            self._process.pid,\n            str(self.build_command()[:3]),\n            len(self._prompt_bytes),\n        )\n\n        return self._process\n\n    _STDIN_CHUNK_SIZE = 64 * 1024  # match typical Linux pipe-buffer size\n\n    def _write_prompt_to_stdin(self, payload: bytes) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Write payload to child stdin in chunks; close stdin in finally.\n\n        Uses os.write on the underlying FD so EINTR can be retried explicitly\n        (Python's BufferedWriter does not surface InterruptedError reliably\n        on partial writes). Closes stdin in finally so claude --print receives\n        EOF even on unexpected exception (BrokenPipe, OSError, etc.). Errors\n        land in self._stdin_error rather than propagating out of start().\n        \"\"\"\n        if self._process is None or self._process.stdin is None:\n            return\n        fd = self._process.stdin.fileno()\n        try:\n            view = memoryview(payload)\n            offset = 0\n            while offset < len(view):\n                chunk = view[offset : offset + self._STDIN_CHUNK_SIZE]\n                while True:\n                    try:\n                        n = os.write(fd, chunk)\n                        break\n                    except InterruptedError:\n                        # EINTR from signal delivery -- retry the same chunk.\n                        continue\n                if n <= 0:\n                    # Defensive -- os.write should not return 0 on a pipe.\n                    break\n                offset += n\n        except BrokenPipeError as exc:\n            # Child exited before reading stdin; surface in wait()/terminate().\n            self._stdin_error = exc\n        except OSError as exc:\n            self._stdin_error = exc\n        finally:\n            try:\n                self._process.stdin.close()\n            except Exception:  # pragma: no cover -- defensive\n                pass\n\n    def wait(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Wait for the process with timeout. Returns exit code.\"\"\"\n        try:\n            self._process.wait(timeout=self.timeout_seconds)\n        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:\n            self.terminate()\n            return 124  # match bash timeout exit code\n\n        rc = self._process.returncode if self._process.returncode is not None else -1\n        if getattr(self, \"_stdin_error\", None) is not None:\n            _log.warning(\n                \"stdin_error pid=%s err=%r\", self._process.pid, self._stdin_error\n            )\n        if self._on_exit is not None:\n            self._on_exit(self._process.pid, rc)\n        self._close_handles()\n        return rc\n\n    def terminate(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Graceful shutdown: SIGTERM, wait 10s, then SIGKILL.\"\"\"\n        if self._process is None or self._process.poll() is not None:\n            self._close_handles()\n            return\n\n        use_pgroup = all(hasattr(os, attr) for attr in (\"getpgid\", \"killpg\"))\n        pgid = os.getpgid(self._process.pid) if use_pgroup else None\n\n        try:\n            if self._on_signal is not None:\n                self._on_signal(self._process.pid, \"SIGTERM\")\n            if use_pgroup and pgid is not None:\n                os.killpg(pgid, signal.SIGTERM)\n            else:\n                self._process.terminate()\n            _log.debug(\"signal_sent SIGTERM pid=%d\", self._process.pid)\n        except ProcessLookupError:\n            self._close_handles()\n            return\n\n        try:\n            self._process.wait(timeout=10)\n        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:\n            try:\n                if use_pgroup and pgid is not None:\n                    os.killpg(pgid, signal.SIGKILL)\n                else:\n                    self._process.kill()\n                _log.debug(\"signal_sent SIGKILL pid=%d\", self._process.pid)\n                self._process.wait(timeout=5)\n            except (ProcessLookupError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):\n                pass\n\n        _log.debug(\n            \"exit pid=%d code=%s\",\n            self._process.pid,\n            self._process.returncode,\n        )\n        if getattr(self, \"_stdin_error\", None) is not None:\n            _log.warning(\n                \"stdin_error pid=%s err=%r\", self._process.pid, self._stdin_error\n            )\n        if self._on_exit is not None:\n            self._on_exit(self._process.pid, self._process.returncode)\n        self._close_handles()\n\n    def validate_tool_write_output(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Check that the LLM wrote the output file via tools.\n\n        Only meaningful when tool_write_mode=True. Returns True if\n        output_file exists and is non-empty.\n        \"\"\"\n        if not self.tool_write_mode:\n            return True\n        if not self.output_file.exists():\n            _log.warning(\n                \"tool_write_mode: output file %s does not exist after subprocess exit\",\n                self.output_file,\n            )\n            return False\n        if self.output_file.stat().st_size == 0:\n            _log.warning(\n                \"tool_write_mode: output file %s is empty after subprocess exit\",\n                self.output_file,\n            )\n            return False\n        return True\n\n    def _close_handles(self) -> None:\n        for fh in (self._stdout_fh, self._stderr_fh):\n            if fh is not None:\n                try:\n                    fh.close()\n                except Exception:\n                    pass\n","numLines":354,"startLine":1,"totalLines":354}}}

Functional Requirements\n37\t\n38\t### FR-1 — PR submission skill with `--monitor` ordinal\n39\t`sc:submit-pr` opens a PR on the fork and arms the in-session monitor at the requested level.\n40\t\n41\t- `FR-1.1` Signature: `/sc:submit-pr [--monitor {0,1,2,3}] [--max-rounds N] [--base master] [--head <branch>] [--title …] [--body …]`\n42\t- `FR-1.2` `--monitor` defaults to `0` (open PR, no monitor — behaviorally identical to today).\n43\t- `FR-1.3` All `gh pr create` / review / reply calls **MUST** pin `--repo IronbellyOrg/IronClaude`.\n44\t- `FR-1.4` Pre-PR checks from `CLAUDE.md` are enforced (confirm `origin`, rebase onto\n45\t  `origin/master` if behind, verify returned URL is `IronbellyOrg/IronClaude`).\n46\t- `FR-1.5` On `--monitor ≥ 1`, after the PR is created the skill arms the Monitor tool (§FR-2).\n47\t\n48\t### FR-2 — In-session Augment review monitor\n49\t- `FR-2.1` Poll the PR for the **Augment Code GitHub App** review using `gh` (e.g.\n50\t  `gh pr view <N> --repo … --json reviews,comments` and\n51\t  `gh api repos/IronbellyOrg/IronClaude/pulls/<N>/comments`).\n52\t- `FR-2.2` Detection contract: distinguish three states — (a) *no review yet* (keep polling),\n53\t  (b) *review posted, zero Medium+ findings* (terminate clean), (c) *review posted with\n54\t  findings* (route). Detection keys on the Augment App's bot login (resolved empirically per\n55\t  OQ1, stored as a config constant, not hard-guessed).\n56\t- `FR-2.3` Poll interval ≥ 30s (GitHub API rate-limit friendly); overall wait bounded by a\n57\t  timeout (default ~30 min, configurable) after which the monitor reports \"review never\n58\t  arrived\" and exits gracefully.\n59\t- `FR-2.4` The monitor is hosted by the **Monitor tool**; the live session must remain open.\n60\t  On session close before review lands, the monitor is lost (documented limitation; V2.0 fixes\n61\t  via headless host).\n62\t\n63\t### FR-3 — Severity → troubleshoot-tier routing\n64\t- `FR-3.1` For each finding, derive a normalized severity (Critical/High/Medium/Low/Nit) by\n65\t  re-grading through the reused **severity rubric** (Augment's self-reported severity is a\n66\t  *hint*, not authoritative — same principle as `sc-auggie-review`).\n67\t- `FR-3.2` Route: **Medium → `/sc:troubleshoot --fix`**; **High or Critical →\n68\t  `/sc:troubleshoot --depth deep --fix`**; **Low / Nit → report only**, no remediation.\n69\t- `FR-3.3` The troubleshoot invocation is seeded with the finding body + `file:line` +\n70\t  evidence so the diagnosis is grounded (no re-deriving the problem from scratch).\n71\t- `FR-3.4` Multiple findings batch sensibly: group by file/area where troubleshoot can address\n72\t  several in one session; never exceed the round budget.\n73\t\n74\t### FR-4 — Autonomy gates (the `--monitor` ordinal)\n75\t- `FR-4.1` **Level 1**: diagnose + propose only. Surface to the user: all Augment comments,\n76\t  the proposed remediation for each, and an explicit offer (\"fix these? y/n\"). **No edits.**\n77\t- `FR-4.2` **Level 2**: implement fixes + validate locally (§FR-5). Then **HALT**: ask the\n78\t  user before any `git commit`/`git push` and before posting any reply. Working tree changes\n79\t  are left in place for inspection.\n80\t- `FR-4.3` **Level 3**: implement + validate + commit + push to the PR branch + reply-to-thread\n81\t  + resolve, unattended — governed by the loop-guard (§FR-6).\n82\t- `FR-4.4` `needs_human_decision`-class findings (ambiguous intent, security trade-offs, API\n83\t  contract changes) **MUST HALT for human sign-off even at level 3** — never auto-ship a\n84\t  guessed default. (Mirrors the repo's \"human-decision items must HALT\" rule.)\n85\t\n86\t### FR-5 — Local validation before push\n87\t- `FR-5.1` \"Validated\" = the relevant test command passes. Default: targeted tests for changed\n88\t  files; escalate to `make test` when changes are cross-cutting (decision recorded per round).\n89\t- `FR-5.2` `make lint` **and** `uv run ruff format --check src/ tests/` must pass before push\n90\t  (green `make lint` ≠ green CI format — known repo gotcha).\n91\t- `FR-5.3` If validation fails, do **not** push; report the failure and (level 3) retry within\n92\t  the round budget or HALT.\n93\t\n94\t### FR-6 — Reply, resolve, and loop termination\n95\t- `FR-6.1` Each implemented fix posts a reply on the **specific** Augment review-comment thread\n96\t  (`gh api …/pulls/<N>/comments/<comment_id>/replies` or the review-comment reply endpoint),\n97\t  summarizing the fix + commit SHA, then resolves the thread.\n98\t- `FR-6.2` **Loop-stop**: after a remediation round + push, wait for Augment's re-review; stop\n99\t  when the re-review surfaces **zero Medium+ findings** OR `--max-rounds` is reached.\n100\t- `FR-6.3` **Loop-guard**: a re-review caused by the monitor's own push counts as the *next*\n101\t  round, not a new independent trigger; the round counter is monotonic and capped. Never exceed\n102\t  `--max-rounds` (default 2, max 5).\n103\t- `FR-6.4` On max-rounds-with-residual-findings, post a summary comment listing unresolved\n104\t  findings and hand back to the human.\n105\t\n106\t### FR-7 — Hook integration (minimal)\n107\t- `FR-7.1` Update `offer-pr-review.sh` to additionally mention `sc:submit-pr --monitor` as the\n108\t  autonomous path (alongside the existing `/sc:auggie-review` offer). Hook stays **fail-open**,\n109\t  never blocks, never spawns anything.\n110\t\n111\t## 3. Non-Functional Requirements\n112\t- `NFR-1` Idempotent replies: never double-post the same fix reply to a thread (track replied\n113\t  comment IDs).\n114\t- `NFR-2` Rate-limit safety: poll ≥30s; back off on `gh` API 403/secondary-limit.\n115\t- `NFR-3` Observability: write a per-run log (`.dev/…/monitor-run-<PR>.jsonl`) of every poll,\n116\t  finding, route decision, fix, push, reply — for forensic review and round auditing.\n117\t- `NFR-4` Fail-safe defaults: unknown severity → treat as Medium (fix tier), not Critical or\n118\t  ignore; unknown bot login → \"review not detected\" (never act on a non-Augment comment).\n119\t- `NFR-5` All paths absolute in user-facing prompts; all paste-ready commands single-line.\n120\t\n121\t## 4. Out of Scope (V1.0)\n122\t- Detached / headless `claude -p` execution host.\n123\t- GitHub Actions / server-side hosting.\n124\t- The @bot-mention → headless trigger (entire V2.0 — separate brainstorm).\n125\t- Reviewing/replying to non-Augment human review comments.\n126\t- Modifying merge state (`--approve` / `--request-changes`) — humans merge.\n127\t\n128\t## 5. Severity → Action Matrix (authoritative)\n129\t\n130\t| Augment finding severity (post-rubric) | Troubleshoot tier | Acts at monitor level |\n131\t|----------------------------------------|-------------------|------------------------|\n132\t| Critical | `/sc:troubleshoot --depth deep --fix` | 1=propose · 2=fix+ask-push · 3=auto (HALT if `needs_human_decision`) |\n133\t| High | `/sc:troubleshoot --depth deep --fix` | same as Critical |\n134\t| Medium | `/sc:troubleshoot --fix` | same gating |\n135\t| Low / Nit | report only | never auto-remediated |\n136\t\n137\t## 6. Acceptance Criteria (testable)\n138\t- `AC-1` `--monitor 0` ⇒ PR opens, zero monitor activity (regression-safe vs today).\n139\t- `AC-2` Given a fixture PR with one Medium + one High Augment finding, level-3 run produces:\n140\t  2 troubleshoot sessions (1 standard, 1 deep), 2 validated fixes, 2 thread replies, 2 resolved\n141\t  threads, ≤`--max-rounds` rounds, deterministic termination.\n142\t- `AC-3` Level-1 run makes **zero** file edits and emits the offer prompt verbatim.\n143\t- `AC-4` Level-2 run leaves changes in the working tree and makes **zero** pushes without\n144\t  approval.\n145\t- `AC-5` A `needs_human_decision` finding HALTs even at level 3.\n146\t- `AC-6` Loop never exceeds `--max-rounds`; a monitor-triggered re-review increments the same\n147\t  counter (no infinite loop) — verified with a 2-round fixture.\n148\t- `AC-7` Every `gh` call in the code path carries `--repo IronbellyOrg/IronClaude`.\n149\t\n150\t## 7. Red-Team — the four risks that can sink this\n151\t\n152\t> These are the points where a naive build breaks. Treated as MUST-resolve-before-build.\n153\t\n154\t- **R1 (OQ1) — Augment review-detection is guesswork until probed.** We do **not** know the\n155\t  Augment App's exact GitHub emission shape (formal review vs. issue comments vs. check-run) or\n156\t  its bot login. **Mitigation:** before writing the parser, run an empirical probe — open one\n157\t  throwaway PR, let Augment review it, capture `gh pr view --json reviews,comments` +\n158\t  `gh api …/pulls/<N>/reviews` + `…/comments`, and lock the detection constant from real data.\n159\t  Building the parser on a guess is the #1 failure mode.\n160\t- **R2 — Loop-guard correctness (infinite remediation).** The monitor pushes a fix → Augment\n161\t  re-reviews → possibly new findings → fix → … A monotonic, capped round counter (FR-6.3) is\n162\t  **load-bearing**; an off-by-one or a \"new review = new trigger\" bug burns compute and spams\n163\t  the PR. **Mitigation:** AC-6 fixture + the round counter must key on \"reviews observed since\n164\t  arm\", not \"reviews seen since last poll\".\n165\t- **R3 — Session-longevity fragility (in-session host).** Augment's review can take minutes;\n166\t  the Monitor tool requires the session to stay open. If the user closes the terminal, the loop\n167\t  silently dies mid-remediation (possibly after a push, before replies). **Mitigation:**\n168\t  checkpoint state to the run-log (NFR-3) so a re-armed monitor can resume; document the\n169\t  limitation prominently; this fragility is the core reason V2.0 moves to a headless host.\n170\t- **R4 — Auto-push blast radius (level 3).** Fully autonomous commit+push to a PR branch is\n171\t  outward-facing and hard to reverse. A wrong fix that passes targeted tests but breaks\n172\t  something untested gets pushed + announced as resolved. **Mitigation:** FR-5.2 lint+format\n173\t  gate, FR-4.4 human-decision HALT, NFR-3 audit log, and a conservative `--max-rounds 2`\n174\t  default. Recommend level 3 be opt-in per-invocation, never a persisted default.\n175\t\n176\t## 8. Build Sequencing (recommended)\n177\t1. **Probe first** (R1): empirical Augment-emission capture → lock detection + severity-parse\n178\t   constants. *Gate before any parser code.*\n179\t2. C1 skill skeleton + `--monitor 0/1` (open PR, poll, report) — no edits, no push.\n180\t3. C3 severity router + troubleshoot wiring (level 1 diagnose-only).\n181\t4. Level 2 (fix-local + validate + ask).\n182\t5. C4 reply-to-thread + resolve, then level 3 + loop-guard (R2) + AC-6 fixture.\n183\t6. C5 hook copy update; C6 full test suite; `make sync-dev` + `make verify-sync`.\n184\t\n185\t## 9. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01N96YzbcHerqQUW9vZmYXAx","type":"tool_result","content":"=== severity-rubric.md exists? ===\n-rw-r--r-- 1 abc abc 10768 May 21 05:20 src/superclaude/skills/sc-auggie-review-protocol/refs/severity-rubric.md\n# Severity Rubric\n\nAuggie's `severity_hint` is a starting point, not authoritative. After Wave 3 grounding completes, every finding is remapped through this rubric so the report grades consistently regardless of which Auggie pass produced the finding.\n\n## Why a rubric (and not just trust Auggie)\n\nSeverity drift is the most common failure mode of AI code review: each invocation grades slightly differently, two findings in the same report contradict each other on severity, and reviewers lose trust in the report. The rubric exists to make grading reproducible — given two findings with the same factual content, they receive the same severity regardless of which pass found them.\n\nThe rubric is intentionally explicit about what each tier means in **decision terms** (block merge? request changes? nit?), not just adjectives. That's what reviewers actually care about.\n\n## The five tiers\n\n### 🔴 Critical — Block merge\n\nA finding is Critical iff at least one of:\n\n- **Exploitable security defect**: anything that lets an unauthenticated or under-authorized actor read, modify, or escalate beyond their intended scope. Examples: SQL injection, missing authz check on a write endpoint, hardcoded production credentials, broken crypto, deserialization of untrusted input into executable types, path traversal in a file-serving endpoint.\n- **Data-integrity defect**: anything that can persist incorrect data, lose data, or corrupt invariants. Examples: missing transaction around a multi-step write, race condition in a counter, off-by-one that miswrites array bounds, a migration that drops a column without a backfill plan.\n- **Crash-on-startup or crash-on-default-path**: a null-deref, unhandled exception, or import-time failure that fires on the most common code path. Not \"this could fail in some edge case\" — \"this will fail on the first run.\"\n- **Regulatory / compliance defect** for a project that operates under one: PII in logs that the project specifically excludes from logs, audit-trail bypass, anything explicitly called out in the repo's compliance docs.\n\nCritical findings receive both a summary comment (in the main report) AND an inline `gh` comment pinned to the file:line.\n\n### 🟠 High — Should fix before merge\n\n=== auggie-review gh posting precedent ===\n30:| **PR** | `<PR-num>` or `<PR-URL>` | `gh pr view <id> --json number,headRefName,baseRefName,headRepository,headRepositoryOwner,title,body,files` |\n56:| `pr_review_url` | string | URL of the posted `gh pr review` comment (empty if `--no-post-pr` or non-PR target) |\n88:   - PR: fetch `gh pr view <id> --json number,headRefName,baseRefName,title,body,files,url,headRepositoryOwner,headRepository` and the merge-base diff range\n117:   - PR: `gh pr diff <id>` → `<output-dir>/diff.patch`\n120:   - PR: from the `files` field of `gh pr view`\n304:   - Summary comment: `gh pr review <PR> --comment --body-file <output-dir>/REVIEW.md`\n307:     gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/pulls/<PR>/comments \\\n314:   - Capture the review URL from `gh pr view <PR> --json reviews -q '.reviews[-1].url'` and record it.\n349:- Use `gh pr review --approve` or `--request-changes` — strictly `--comment`\n365:| `gh pr review` post fails | Save the body to `<output-dir>/REVIEW.md`, surface the path to the user, status `partial` | None |","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"537c99c9-4a80-40a2-a8f0-3491a13df75d","uuid":"1f9a16b5-64b8-4f1d-85d2-8e20dbf59b8f","timestamp":"2026-06-11T12:24:57.856Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"=== severity-rubric.md exists? ===\n-rw-r--r-- 1 abc abc 10768 May 21 05:20 src/superclaude/skills/sc-auggie-review-protocol/refs/severity-rubric.md\n# Severity Rubric\n\nAuggie's `severity_hint` is a starting point, not authoritative. After Wave 3 grounding completes, every finding is remapped through this rubric so the report grades consistently regardless of which Auggie pass produced the finding.\n\n## Why a rubric (and not just trust Auggie)\n\nSeverity drift is the most common failure mode of AI code review: each invocation grades slightly differently, two findings in the same report contradict each other on severity, and reviewers lose trust in the report. The rubric exists to make grading reproducible — given two findings with the same factual content, they receive the same severity regardless of which pass found them.\n\nThe rubric is intentionally explicit about what each tier means in **decision terms** (block merge? request changes? nit?), not just adjectives. That's what reviewers actually care about.\n\n## The five tiers\n\n### 🔴 Critical — Block merge\n\nA finding is Critical iff at least one of:\n\n- **Exploitable security defect**: anything that lets an unauthenticated or under-authorized actor read, modify, or escalate beyond their intended scope. Examples: SQL injection, missing authz check on a write endpoint, hardcoded production credentials, broken crypto, deserialization of untrusted input into executable types, path traversal in a file-serving endpoint.\n- **Data-integrity defect**: anything that can persist incorrect data, lose data, or corrupt invariants. Examples: missing transaction around a multi-step write, race condition in a counter, off-by-one that miswrites array bounds, a migration that drops a column without a backfill plan.\n- **Crash-on-startup or crash-on-default-path**: a null-deref, unhandled exception, or import-time failure that fires on the most common code path. Not \"this could fail in some edge case\" — \"this will fail on the first run.\"\n- **Regulatory / compliance defect** for a project that operates under one: PII in logs that the project specifically excludes from logs, audit-trail bypass, anything explicitly called out in the repo's compliance docs.\n\nCritical findings receive both a summary comment (in the main report) AND an inline `gh` comment pinned to the file:line.\n\n### 🟠 High — Should fix before merge\n\n=== auggie-review gh posting precedent ===\n30:| **PR** | `<PR-num>` or `<PR-URL>` | `gh pr view <id> --json number,headRefName,baseRefName,headRepository,headRepositoryOwner,title,body,files` |\n56:| `pr_review_url` | string | URL of the posted `gh pr review` comment (empty if `--no-post-pr` or non-PR target) |\n88:   - PR: fetch `gh pr view <id> --json number,headRefName,baseRefName,title,body,files,url,headRepositoryOwner,headRepository` and the merge-base diff range\n117:   - PR: `gh pr diff <id>` → `<output-dir>/diff.patch`\n120:   - PR: from the `files` field of `gh pr view`\n304:   - Summary comment: `gh pr review <PR> --comment --body-file <output-dir>/REVIEW.md`\n307:     gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/pulls/<PR>/comments \\\n314:   - Capture the review URL from `gh pr view <PR> --json reviews -q '.reviews[-1].url'` and record it.\n349:- Use `gh pr review --approve` or `--request-changes` — strictly `--comment`\n365:| `gh pr review` post fails | Save the body to `<output-dir>/REVIEW.md`, surface the path to the user, status `partial` | None |","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false}}

← full parent environment, INCLUDING secrets\nenv.pop(\"CLAUDECODE\", None)\nenv.pop(\"CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT\", None)\nif env_vars:\n    env.update(env_vars)         # ← MERGE/override semantics — ADD only, cannot REMOVE\nreturn env\n```\n\nThe `env_vars` kwarg uses **`.update()` (additive)**, so it can inject vars but **cannot\nstrip** an inherited secret (`GH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, push token) from the child.\nThe Runner's INV-001/SC-7 requirement (\"no `GH_TOKEN`, no push credential in the Runner env\")\n**cannot be met by passing `env_vars` alone** — it requires either a new `build_env`\ncode path that starts from an *empty* dict + allowlist, or running the Runner in a sandbox\nwhose parent process already lacks the secrets (the §6 sandbox boundary). The spec's caveat is\n**correct and load-bearing**; this is the single most important nuance in the whole Reuse Map.\n**Recommendation:** add an `env_mode=\"allowlist\"` branch to `build_env()` (start from `{}`),\nor have the sandbox's init process hold no secrets — do NOT rely on `env_vars` override.\n\n### H1/H2 — swarm \"bounded-counter\" (`cli/swarm/commands.py:2269`)  **[CODE-CONTRADICTED — citation mis-describes the code]**\n\n§9 claims: *\"Counter mirrors swarm bounded-counter (`cli/swarm/commands.py:2269`):\n**monotonic, disk-authoritative, survives restarts**.\"* The code at lines 2255-2290 is the\n**`watch` sub-command's in-memory poll loop**:\n\n```python\niterations += 1\nif watch_max_iterations is not None and iterations >= watch_max_iterations:\n    break\ntime.sleep(watch_interval)\n```\n\n`iterations` is a **local variable in a CLI watch loop** — it is *not* disk-persisted, *not*\nrestart-surviving, and *not* a per-PR/per-thread round ledger. The \"monotonic,\ndisk-authoritative, survives restarts\" properties §9 attributes to this line **do not exist\nat that citation.** The disk-authoritative round/budget counter the spec needs is **net-new\nwork**, not a reuse. Also probe whether a `cli/remediate/` package already exists.\n**Status:** In Progress\n**Date:** 2026-06-11\n\n---\n\n## Investigation Rationale\n\nThe research-notes contained no Agent 5 block, so this agent takes the broad mandate.\nThe single highest-leverage broad investigation for THIS PRD is **verifying the reuse\nclaims** — the merged-requirements spec hangs its entire build sequencing (§19) and\ncomponent inventory (§2) on a small set of existing primitives it promises to \"Reuse\".\nIf those primitives are mis-cited (wrong line, wrong signature, different behavior than\ndescribed), the whole build plan inherits the error. This agent code-verifies each one.\n\n**De-confliction note:** all 8 parallel agents independently chose \"reuse-map verification\"\nas their scope (Agents 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 8 headers all cite `ClaudeProcess`/swarm-counter/\nseverity-rubric). To avoid 8 redundant reports, this agent does a *fast self-contained\nconfirm* of the 3 over-covered primitives, then spends the bulk of its effort on two\n**under-covered** areas: (A) the **V1.0 `pr_submit` prior-art package that the V2 Reuse Map\nomits**, and (B) **net-new surfaces with ZERO codebase precedent** (GraphQL\n`resolveReviewThread`, ETag/304 polling, `deploy/`+systemd, the Python `gh` wrapper).\n\n---\n\n## Part 1 — Fast confirm of the over-covered reuse primitives\n\n### R2 — `ClaudeProcess` (`cli/pipeline/process.py:72`)  **[CODE-VERIFIED]**\n\n- Class **is** declared at line 72 (`process.py:72`). Exact citation correct.\n- `build_command()` (`process.py:121`) emits exactly what §7 claims:\n  `claude --print --verbose <permission_flag> --no-session-persistence --tools default\n  --max-turns N --output-format <fmt>` (+ optional `--model`, + `extra_args`). **Matches.**\n- Default `permission_flag=\"--dangerously-skip-permissions\"` (`process.py:93`) — §7's claim\n  that this is the default is correct. **Matches.**\n- Prompt delivered via **stdin** (`_write_prompt_to_stdin`, `process.py:221`), chunked\n  64 KiB, EINTR-retried, closes stdin in `finally`. §7's \"prompt delivered via stdin\n  (bypasses 128KB argv limit)\" is **accurate and already hardened** (commit 1b0264f1 in this\n  branch's log is exactly this work). **Matches — better than claimed.**\n- Constructor already has an `env_vars: dict|None` param (`process.py:100`) and\n  `timeout_seconds` (default 6300, `process.py:94`) — §14's `ClaudeProcess.timeout_seconds`\n  reference is valid.\n\n### R2 env-allowlist gap — §7's central reuse caveat is **[CODE-VERIFIED as a real gap]**\n\n§7 insists: *\"`build_env()` MUST be wrapped with an explicit allowlist `env_vars` (not the\ncurrent full `os.environ.copy()`).\"* Reading `build_env()` (`process.py:145-160`):\n\n```python\nenv = os.environ.copy()          # full parent environment, INCLUDING secrets\nenv.pop(\"CLAUDECODE\", None)\nenv.pop(\"CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT\", None)\nif env_vars:\n    env.update(env_vars)         # MERGE/override semantics -- ADD only, cannot REMOVE\nreturn env\n```\n\nThe `env_vars` kwarg uses **`.update()` (additive)**, so it can inject vars but **cannot\nstrip** an inherited secret (`GH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, push token) from the child.\nThe Runner's INV-001/SC-7 requirement (\"no `GH_TOKEN`, no push credential in the Runner env\")\n**cannot be met by passing `env_vars` alone** — it requires either a new `build_env`\ncode path that starts from an *empty* dict + allowlist, or running the Runner in a sandbox\nwhose parent process already lacks the secrets (the §6 sandbox boundary). The spec's caveat is\n**correct and load-bearing**; this is the single most important nuance in the whole Reuse Map.\n**Recommendation:** add an `env_mode=\"allowlist\"` branch to `build_env()` (start from `{}`),\nor have the sandbox's init process hold no secrets — do NOT rely on `env_vars` override.\n\n### H1/H2 — swarm \"bounded-counter\" (`cli/swarm/commands.py:2269`)  **[CODE-CONTRADICTED — citation mis-describes the code]**\n\n§9 claims: *\"Counter mirrors swarm bounded-counter (`cli/swarm/commands.py:2269`):\n**monotonic, disk-authoritative, survives restarts**.\"* The code at lines 2255-2290 is the\n**`watch` sub-command's in-memory poll loop**:\n\n```python\niterations += 1\nif watch_max_iterations is not None and iterations >= watch_max_iterations:\n    break\ntime.sleep(watch_interval)\n```\n\n`iterations` is a **local variable in a CLI watch loop** — it is *not* disk-persisted, *not*\nrestart-surviving, and *not* a per-PR/per-thread round ledger. The \"monotonic,\ndisk-authoritative, survives restarts\" properties §9 attributes to this line **do not exist\nat that citation.** The disk-authoritative round/budget counter the spec needs is **net-new\nwork**, not a reuse. (See Part 2 — V1's `pr_submit/loop_guard` + `run_log` are the *actual*\nnearest prior art, and the spec cites neither.) **Action for TDD:** drop the swarm:2269\ncitation or downgrade it to \"iteration-cap idiom only\"; source the durable counter from\n`pr_submit` instead.\n\n### S1 — severity rubric (`sc-auggie-review-protocol/refs/severity-rubric.md`)  **[CODE-VERIFIED]**\n\nFile exists (10,768 bytes, 5 explicit tiers with decision-term definitions:\nCritical=block merge … Nit). §17's \"re-grade via the rubric, Augment severity is a hint\"\nis the rubric's own stated purpose (\"`severity_hint` is a starting point, not\nauthoritative\"). **Reuse claim valid.**\n\n### H4 precedent — `gh` posting in `sc-auggie-review-protocol/SKILL.md`  **[CODE-VERIFIED, partial]**\n\n- Inline-comment precedent confirmed: `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/pulls/<PR>/comments`\n  (SKILL.md:307) — a valid template for H4's reply body POST.\n- Summary precedent: `gh pr review <PR> --comment --body-file …` (SKILL.md:304).\n- Guardrail precedent: SKILL.md:349 enforces `--comment` **only**, *never*\n  `--approve`/`--request-changes` — exactly matches V2 §20 \"modifying merge state out of\n  scope.\" Good reusable discipline.\n- **BUT** there is **no `/replies` endpoint and no GraphQL `resolveReviewThread`** anywhere\n  in auggie-review (or `src/`). So H4's *reply-to-thread* + *resolve* halves are genuinely\n  net-new (consistent with spec §12 \"net-new, absent from repo today\" and V1 C4 \"New\").\n\n**Key Takeaways (Part 1)**\n- `ClaudeProcess` reuse is **solid** — citation, signature, and stdin behavior all verified.\n- The **env-allowlist caveat is real**: `env_vars` is additive and cannot strip secrets;\n  INV-001/SC-7 needs a new empty-base `build_env` path or a secret-free sandbox parent.\n- The **swarm:2269 counter citation is wrong** — it's an in-memory watch-loop cap, not the\n  durable round ledger §9 describes. Durable counter = net-new (or reuse V1 `loop_guard`).\n- Severity rubric + gh inline-comment precedents are **verified**; reply/resolve are Also probe whether a `cli/remediate/` package already exists.\n**Status:** In Progress\n**Date:** 2026-06-11\n\n---\n\n## Investigation Rationale\n\nThe research-notes contained no Agent 5 block, so this agent takes the broad mandate.\nThe single highest-leverage broad investigation for THIS PRD is **verifying the reuse\nclaims** — the merged-requirements spec hangs its entire build sequencing (§19) and\ncomponent inventory (§2) on a small set of existing primitives it promises to \"Reuse\".\nIf those primitives are mis-cited (wrong line, wrong signature, different behavior than\ndescribed), the whole build plan inherits the error. This agent code-verifies each one.\n\n**De-confliction note:** all 8 parallel agents independently chose \"reuse-map verification\"\nas their scope (Agents 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7, 8 headers all cite `ClaudeProcess`/swarm-counter/\nseverity-rubric). To avoid 8 redundant reports, this agent does a *fast self-contained\nconfirm* of the 3 over-covered primitives, then spends the bulk of its effort on two\n**under-covered** areas: (A) the **V1.0 `pr_submit` prior-art package that the V2 Reuse Map\nomits**, and (B) **net-new surfaces with ZERO codebase precedent** (GraphQL\n`resolveReviewThread`, ETag/304 polling, `deploy/`+systemd, the Python `gh` wrapper).\n\n---\n\n## Part 1 — Fast confirm of the over-covered reuse primitives\n\n### R2 — `ClaudeProcess` (`cli/pipeline/process.py:72`)  **[CODE-VERIFIED]**\n\n- Class **is** declared at line 72 (`process.py:72`). Exact citation correct.\n- `build_command()` (`process.py:121`) emits exactly what §7 claims:\n  `claude --print --verbose <permission_flag> --no-session-persistence --tools default\n  --max-turns N --output-format <fmt>` (+ optional `--model`, + `extra_args`). **Matches.**\n- Default `permission_flag=\"--dangerously-skip-permissions\"` (`process.py:93`) — §7's claim\n  that this is the default is correct. **Matches.**\n- Prompt delivered via **stdin** (`_write_prompt_to_stdin`, `process.py:221`), chunked\n  64 KiB, EINTR-retried, closes stdin in `finally`. §7's \"prompt delivered via stdin\n  (bypasses 128KB argv limit)\" is **accurate and already hardened** (commit 1b0264f1 in this\n  branch's log is exactly this work). **Matches — better than claimed.**\n- Constructor already has an `env_vars: dict|None` param (`process.py:100`) and\n  `timeout_seconds` (default 6300, `process.py:94`) — §14's `ClaudeProcess.timeout_seconds`\n  reference is valid.\n\n### R2 env-allowlist gap — §7's central reuse caveat is **[CODE-VERIFIED as a real gap]**\n\n§7 insists: *\"`build_env()` MUST be wrapped with an explicit allowlist `env_vars` (not the\ncurrent full `os.environ.copy()`).\"* Reading `build_env()` (`process.py:145-160`):\n\n```python\nenv = os.environ.copy()          # full parent environment, INCLUDING secrets\nenv.pop(\"CLAUDECODE\", None)\nenv.pop(\"CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT\", None)\nif env_vars:\n    env.update(env_vars)         # MERGE/override semantics -- ADD only, cannot REMOVE\nreturn env\n```\n\nThe `env_vars` kwarg uses **`.update()` (additive)**, so it can inject vars but **cannot\nstrip** an inherited secret (`GH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, push token) from the child.\nThe Runner's INV-001/SC-7 requirement (\"no `GH_TOKEN`, no push credential in the Runner env\")\n**cannot be met by passing `env_vars` alone** — it requires either a new `build_env`\ncode path that starts from an *empty* dict + allowlist, or running the Runner in a sandbox\nwhose parent process already lacks the secrets (the §6 sandbox boundary). The spec's caveat is\n**correct and load-bearing**; this is the single most important nuance in the whole Reuse Map.\n**Recommendation:** add an `env_mode=\"allowlist\"` branch to `build_env()` (start from `{}`),\nor have the sandbox's init process hold no secrets — do NOT rely on `env_vars` override.\n\n### H1/H2 — swarm \"bounded-counter\" (`cli/swarm/commands.py:2269`)  **[CODE-CONTRADICTED — citation mis-describes the code]**\n\n§9 claims: *\"Counter mirrors swarm bounded-counter (`cli/swarm/commands.py:2269`):\n**monotonic, disk-authoritative, survives restarts**.\"* The code at lines 2255-2290 is the\n**`watch` sub-command's in-memory poll loop**:\n\n```python\niterations += 1\nif watch_max_iterations is not None and iterations >= watch_max_iterations:\n    break\ntime.sleep(watch_interval)\n```\n\n`iterations` is a **local variable in a CLI watch loop** — it is *not* disk-persisted, *not*\nrestart-surviving, and *not* a per-PR/per-thread round ledger. The \"monotonic,\ndisk-authoritative, survives restarts\" properties §9 attributes to this line **do not exist\nat that citation.** The disk-authoritative round/budget counter the spec needs is **net-new\nwork**, not a reuse. (See Part 2 — V1's `pr_submit/loop_guard` + `run_log` are the *actual*\nnearest prior art, and the spec cites neither.) **Action for TDD:** drop the swarm:2269\ncitation or downgrade it to \"iteration-cap idiom only\"; source the durable counter from\n`pr_submit` instead.\n\n### S1 — severity rubric (`sc-auggie-review-protocol/refs/severity-rubric.md`)  **[CODE-VERIFIED]**\n\nFile exists (10,768 bytes, 5 explicit tiers with decision-term definitions:\nCritical=block merge … Nit). §17's \"re-grade via the rubric, Augment severity is a hint\"\nis the rubric's own stated purpose (\"`severity_hint` is a starting point, not\nauthoritative\"). **Reuse claim valid.**\n\n### H4 precedent — `gh` posting in `sc-auggie-review-protocol/SKILL.md`  **[CODE-VERIFIED, partial]**\n\n- Inline-comment precedent confirmed: `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/pulls/<PR>/comments`\n  (SKILL.md:307) — a valid template for H4's reply body POST.\n- Summary precedent: `gh pr review <PR> --comment --body-file …` (SKILL.md:304).\n- Guardrail precedent: SKILL.md:349 enforces `--comment` **only**, *never*\n  `--approve`/`--request-changes` — exactly matches V2 §20 \"modifying merge state out of\n  scope.\" Good reusable discipline.\n- **BUT** there is **no `/replies` endpoint and no GraphQL `resolveReviewThread`** anywhere\n  in auggie-review (or `src/`). So H4's *reply-to-thread* + *resolve* halves are genuinely\n  net-new (consistent with spec §12 \"net-new, absent from repo today\" and V1 C4 \"New\").\n\n**Key Takeaways (Part 1)**\n- `ClaudeProcess` reuse is **solid** — citation, signature, and stdin behavior all verified.\n- The **env-allowlist caveat is real**: `env_vars` is additive and cannot strip secrets;\n  INV-001/SC-7 needs a new empty-base `build_env` path or a secret-free sandbox parent.\n- The **swarm:2269 counter citation is wrong** — it's an in-memory watch-loop cap, not the\n  durable round ledger §9 describes. Durable counter = net-new (or reuse V1 `loop_guard`).\n- Severity rubric + gh inline-comment precedents are **verified**; reply/resolve are net-new.\n\n---\n\n## Part 2 — THE KEY FINDING: V1.0 Exact citation correct.\n- `build_command()` (`process.py:121`) emits exactly what §7 claims:\n  `claude --print --verbose <permission_flag> --no-session-persistence --tools default\n  --max-turns N --output-format <fmt>` (+ optional `--model`, + `extra_args`). **Matches.**\n- Default `permission_flag=\"--dangerously-skip-permissions\"` (`process.py:93`) — §7's claim\n  that this is the default is correct. **Matches.**\n- Prompt delivered via **stdin** (`_write_prompt_to_stdin`, `process.py:221`), chunked\n  64 KiB, EINTR-retried, closes stdin in `finally`. §7's \"prompt delivered via stdin\n  (bypasses 128KB argv limit)\" is **accurate and already hardened** (commit 1b0264f1 in this\n  branch's log is exactly this work). **Matches — better than claimed.**\n- Constructor already has an `env_vars: dict|None` param (`process.py:100`) and\n  `timeout_seconds` (default 6300, `process.py:94`) — §14's `ClaudeProcess.timeout_seconds`\n  reference is valid.\n\n### R2 env-allowlist gap — §7's central reuse caveat is **[CODE-VERIFIED as a real gap]**\n\n§7 insists: *\"`build_env()` MUST be wrapped with an explicit allowlist `env_vars` (not the\ncurrent full `os.environ.copy()`).\"* Reading `build_env()` (`process.py:145-160`):\n\n```python\nenv = os.environ.copy()          # full parent environment, INCLUDING secrets\nenv.pop(\"CLAUDECODE\", None)\nenv.pop(\"CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT\", None)\nif env_vars:\n    env.update(env_vars)         # MERGE/override semantics -- ADD only, cannot REMOVE\nreturn env\n```\n\nThe `env_vars` kwarg uses **`.update()` (additive)**, so it can inject vars but **cannot\nstrip** an inherited secret (`GH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, push token) from the child.\nThe Runner's INV-001/SC-7 requirement (\"no `GH_TOKEN`, no push credential in the Runner env\")\n**cannot be met by passing `env_vars` alone** — it requires either a new `build_env`\ncode path that starts from an *empty* dict + allowlist, or running the Runner in a sandbox\nwhose parent process already lacks the secrets (the §6 sandbox boundary). The spec's caveat is\n**correct and load-bearing**; this is the single most important nuance in the whole Reuse Map.\n**Recommendation:** add an `env_mode=\"allowlist\"` branch to `build_env()` (start from `{}`),\nor have the sandbox's init process hold no secrets — do NOT rely on `env_vars` override.\n\n### H1/H2 — swarm \"bounded-counter\" (`cli/swarm/commands.py:2269`)  **[CODE-CONTRADICTED — citation mis-describes the code]**\n\n§9 claims: *\"Counter mirrors swarm bounded-counter (`cli/swarm/commands.py:2269`):\n**monotonic, disk-authoritative, survives restarts**.\"* The code at lines 2255-2290 is the\n**`watch` sub-command's in-memory poll loop**:\n\n```python\niterations += 1\nif watch_max_iterations is not None and iterations >= watch_max_iterations:\n    break\ntime.sleep(watch_interval)\n```\n\n`iterations` is a **local variable in a CLI watch loop** — it is *not* disk-persisted, *not*\nrestart-surviving, and *not* a per-PR/per-thread round ledger. The \"monotonic,\ndisk-authoritative, survives restarts\" properties §9 attributes to this line **do not exist\nat that citation.** The disk-authoritative round/budget counter the spec needs is **net-new\nwork**, not a reuse. So H4's *reply-to-thread* + *resolve* halves are genuinely\n  net-new (consistent with spec §12 \"net-new, absent from repo today\" and V1 C4 \"New\").\n\n**Key Takeaways (Part 1)**\n- `ClaudeProcess` reuse is **solid** — citation, signature, and stdin behavior all verified.\n- The **env-allowlist caveat is real**: `env_vars` is additive and cannot strip secrets;\n  INV-001/SC-7 needs a new empty-base `build_env` path or a secret-free sandbox parent.\n- The **swarm:2269 counter citation is wrong** — it's an in-memory watch-loop cap, not the\n  durable round ledger §9 describes. Exact citation correct.\n- `build_command()` (`process.py:121`) emits exactly what §7 claims:\n  `claude --print --verbose <permission_flag> --no-session-persistence --tools default\n  --max-turns N --output-format <fmt>` (+ optional `--model`, + `extra_args`). **Matches.**\n- Default `permission_flag=\"--dangerously-skip-permissions\"` (`process.py:93`) — §7's claim\n  that this is the default is correct. **Matches.**\n- Prompt delivered via **stdin** (`_write_prompt_to_stdin`, `process.py:221`), chunked\n  64 KiB, EINTR-retried, closes stdin in `finally`. §7's \"prompt delivered via stdin\n  (bypasses 128KB argv limit)\" is **accurate and already hardened** (commit 1b0264f1 in this\n  branch's log is exactly this work). **Matches — better than claimed.**\n- Constructor already has an `env_vars: dict|None` param (`process.py:100`) and\n  `timeout_seconds` (default 6300, `process.py:94`) — §14's `ClaudeProcess.timeout_seconds`\n  reference is valid.\n\n### R2 env-allowlist gap — §7's central reuse caveat is **[CODE-VERIFIED as a real gap]**\n\n§7 insists: *\"`build_env()` MUST be wrapped with an explicit allowlist `env_vars` (not the\ncurrent full `os.environ.copy()`).\"* Reading `build_env()` (`process.py:145-160`):\n\n```python\nenv = os.environ.copy()          # full parent environment, INCLUDING secrets\nenv.pop(\"CLAUDECODE\", None)\nenv.pop(\"CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT\", None)\nif env_vars:\n    env.update(env_vars)         # MERGE/override semantics -- ADD only, cannot REMOVE\nreturn env\n```\n\nThe `env_vars` kwarg uses **`.update()` (additive)**, so it can inject vars but **cannot\nstrip** an inherited secret (`GH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, push token) from the child.\nThe Runner's INV-001/SC-7 requirement (\"no `GH_TOKEN`, no push credential in the Runner env\")\n**cannot be met by passing `env_vars` alone** — it requires either a new `build_env`\ncode path that starts from an *empty* dict + allowlist, or running the Runner in a sandbox\nwhose parent process already lacks the secrets (the §6 sandbox boundary). The spec's caveat is\n**correct and load-bearing**; this is the single most important nuance in the whole Reuse Map.\n**Recommendation:** add an `env_mode=\"allowlist\"` branch to `build_env()` (start from `{}`),\nor have the sandbox's init process hold no secrets — do NOT rely on `env_vars` override.\n\n### H1/H2 — swarm \"bounded-counter\" (`cli/swarm/commands.py:2269`)  **[CODE-CONTRADICTED — citation mis-describes the code]**\n\n§9 claims: *\"Counter mirrors swarm bounded-counter (`cli/swarm/commands.py:2269`):\n**monotonic, disk-authoritative, survives restarts**.\"* The code at lines 2255-2290 is the\n**`watch` sub-command's in-memory poll loop**:\n\n```python\niterations += 1\nif watch_max_iterations is not None and iterations >= watch_max_iterations:\n    break\ntime.sleep(watch_interval)\n```\n\n`iterations` is a **local variable in a CLI watch loop** — it is *not* disk-persisted, *not*\nrestart-surviving, and *not* a per-PR/per-thread round ledger. The \"monotonic,\ndisk-authoritative, survives restarts\" properties §9 attributes to this line **do not exist\nat that citation.** The disk-authoritative round/budget counter the spec needs is **net-new\nwork**, not a reuse. (See Part 2 — V1's `pr_submit/loop_guard` + `run_log` are the *actual*\nnearest prior art, and the spec cites neither.) **Action for TDD:** drop the swarm:2269\ncitation or downgrade it to \"iteration-cap idiom only\"; source the durable counter from\n`pr_submit` instead.\n\n### S1 — severity rubric (`sc-auggie-review-protocol/refs/severity-rubric.md`)  **[CODE-VERIFIED]**\n\nFile exists (10,768 bytes, 5 explicit tiers with decision-term definitions:\nCritical=block merge … Nit). §17's \"re-grade via the rubric, Augment severity is a hint\"\nis the rubric's own stated purpose (\"`severity_hint` is a starting point, not\nauthoritative\"). **Reuse claim valid.**\n\n### H4 precedent — `gh` posting in `sc-auggie-review-protocol/SKILL.md`  **[CODE-VERIFIED, partial]**\n\n- Inline-comment precedent confirmed: `gh api repos/<owner>/<repo>/pulls/<PR>/comments`\n  (SKILL.md:307) — a valid template for H4's reply body POST.\n- Summary precedent: `gh pr review <PR> --comment --body-file …` (SKILL.md:304).\n- Guardrail precedent: SKILL.md:349 enforces `--comment` **only**, *never*\n  `--approve`/`--request-changes` — exactly matches V2 §20 \"modifying merge state out of\n  scope.\" Good reusable discipline.\n- **BUT** there is **no `/replies` endpoint and no GraphQL `resolveReviewThread`** anywhere\n  in auggie-review (or `src/`). So H4's *reply-to-thread* + *resolve* halves are genuinely\n  net-new (consistent with spec §12 \"net-new, absent from repo today\" and V1 C4 \"New\").\n\n**Key Takeaways (Part 1)**\n- `ClaudeProcess` reuse is **solid** — citation, signature, and stdin behavior all verified.\n- The **env-allowlist caveat is real**: `env_vars` is additive and cannot strip secrets;\n  INV-001/SC-7 needs a new empty-base `build_env` path or a secret-free sandbox parent.\n- The **swarm:2269 counter citation is wrong** — it's an in-memory watch-loop cap, not the\n  durable round ledger §9 describes. Durable counter = net-new (or reuse V1 `loop_guard`).\n- Severity rubric + gh inline-comment precedents are **verified**; reply/resolve are net-new.\n\n---\n\n## Part 2 — THE KEY FINDING: V1.0 The pure core is host-agnostic and ports cleanly; the I/O edges are rewritten.\n\nSo the correct framing for the PRD/TDD is **\"extract-and-extend `pr_submit`'s pure core into\n`cli/remediate/`\"**, Part 3 — Second uncited reuse: `cli/roadmap/remediate_executor.py` (the closest analog to V2's R2/R4)\n\nThe V2 Reuse Map cites the **raw** `ClaudeProcess` primitive but misses\n`src/superclaude/cli/roadmap/remediate_executor.py` — an existing **agent-driven remediation\norchestrator** that already sits one abstraction level closer to V2's Runner/executor/sandbox.\n**[CODE-VERIFIED]** Its module docstring + signatures show:\n\n- **It already composes `ClaudeProcess`** (`remediate_executor.py:26`) to run remediation\n  agents in parallel (`ThreadPoolExecutor`, `_run_agent_for_file`, `_run_agent_with_retry`).\n  This is a *working* example of the exact \"spawn `claude -p` to remediate a finding\" pattern\n  V2 R2 needs — not just the primitive, but the primitive *in remediation use*.\n- **`enforce_allowlist(findings) -> (allowed, rejected)`** (`remediate_executor.py:155`) — a\n  **pure** (NFR-004) file allowlist gate: any finding touching a file outside `EDITABLE_FILES`\n  is SKIPPED with a warning. This is a direct analog of V2 §6's \"never modify files outside the\n  workspace\" file-write boundary. The *reject-by-default + log* shape is reusable for the\n  sandbox's write-scope enforcement.\n- **`create_snapshots` / `restore_from_snapshots` / `cleanup_snapshots`** (atomic\n  read→tmp→`os.replace`, NFR-005) + **per-file rollback** (`_handle_file_rollback`,\n  `remediate_executor.py:431`) — the snapshot/rollback discipline V2's sandbox checkout +\n  per-PR `flock` tree-mutation (§9) and \"validation fails → don't push\" (§8) want.\n- **`check_patch_diff_size` / `_check_diff_size`** (`remediate_executor.py:309`) — a patch-size\n  guard relevant to V2's push blast-radius / budget concerns (R4 §9 INV-009/018).\n- **`_check_cross_file_coherence`** (`remediate_executor.py:453`) — multi-file fix coherence,\n  relevant to V2 fix-level validation (SC-4).\n- **`RemediationPatch` dataclass (MorphLLM-compatible)** + **`fallback_apply()`** deterministic\n  text replacement (`remediate_executor.py:643`) — analog to V2 §3 step (j) \"emit patch-bundle.\"\n- **`update_remediation_tasklist`** writes back **atomically** (tmp + `os.replace`, NFR-005,\n  `remediate_executor.py:563-589`).\n\n**Verdict:** `remediate_executor.py` is **mirror-shape / extract-shared** for V2's R2 executor,\nR4 sandbox-allowlist, and the patch-emit/rollback path. The Reuse Map should cite it alongside\n`ClaudeProcess`. Caveat: it edits a **fixed `EDITABLE_FILES` allowlist** in-process (roadmap\nremediation has a known target set), whereas V2's Runner edits an **arbitrary PR-head\ncheckout** — so the allowlist becomes \"stay inside the sandbox workspace,\" not a named file\nset. The *mechanism* ports; the *policy* widens.\n\n### Atomic-write precedent for the H1/§10 ledger  **[CODE-VERIFIED]**\n\n§10 specifies \"atomic writes (temp + `os.rename`), append-only with `O_APPEND`.\" Two proven\ntemplates already exist:\n\n- **`cli/recommend/cache.py:save()`** (`cache.py:127-160`) — crash-safe atomic write:\n  *randomized same-directory* temp name (`.{name}.tmp.{pid}.{id}`) + `os.replace` +\n  `finally`-cleanup if `os.replace` never ran. Its own comment calls out bounding the\n  *\"worktree-concurrency last-write-wins window\"* — directly relevant to the §9 per-PR `flock`\n  requirement and the repo's known \"parallel sessions share the git index\" hazard.\n- **`cli/roadmap/remediate_executor.py`** uses `os.replace` for every snapshot/restore/tasklist\n  write (≥8 call sites). Consistent house style: **`os.replace`, not `os.rename`** (atomic on\n  POSIX, and atomic-overwrite on Windows where `os.rename` is not).\n\n**Recommendation for TDD:** the §10 ledger should follow `cache.py`'s randomized-tmp +\n`os.replace` + `finally`-cleanup pattern (not the bare `os.rename` the spec names), and combine\nit with `O_APPEND` for the JSONL event stream. Both halves have a verified in-repo precedent.\n\n**Key Takeaways (Part 3)**\n- `roadmap/remediate_executor.py` is the **single closest existing analog** to V2's R2/R4 —\n  it already runs `ClaudeProcess` for remediation with allowlist + snapshot-rollback + retry +\n  patch-apply. The Reuse Map omits it; it should be the *primary* executor reuse citation.\n- The ledger's \"atomic write\" is **already solved twice** in-repo (`cache.py`,\n  `remediate_executor.py`) with `os.replace` (not `os.rename`) + randomized tmp + crash cleanup.\n\n---\n\n## Part 4 — Net-new surfaces with ZERO codebase precedent (effort/risk concentrators)\n\nThese are the components where the build has **no in-repo prior art at all** — they dominate\nthe genuine net-new effort and risk. Net: V2 correctly identifies the *conceptual* lineage but under-credits the *code* lineage.\n\n---\n\n## Gaps and Questions\n\n1. **Reuse Map omits `pr_submit` and `remediate_executor.py`.** The two largest reuse\n   opportunities (V1 decision core; the existing `ClaudeProcess`-driven remediation executor)\n   are absent from §2 / the Reuse Map. **Q for TDD:** extend `pr_submit`'s pure core into\n   `cli/remediate/`, or greenfield? (This agent's evidence says extract-and-extend.)\n2. **swarm:2269 mis-citation.** §9 attributes \"monotonic, disk-authoritative, survives\n   restarts\" to an in-memory watch-loop counter. The durable counter is net-new. Fix the\n   citation before it propagates into the TDD/tasklist.\n3. **`build_env` cannot strip secrets.** INV-001/SC-7 needs either a new empty-base\n   allowlist `build_env` path or a secret-free sandbox parent — `env_vars` override alone is\n   insufficient. Is this slated as a `process.py` change or a sandbox-only guarantee?\n4. **H5 has no Python precedent and current `--repo` safety is prose-only.** Confirm H5 is\n   built and unit-tested *before* any `gh`-calling code (spec §19.2 already sequences this —\n   reinforce it). A single un-wrapped `gh` call in the daemon re-introduces the upstream-PR\n   hazard the CLAUDE.md rule exists to prevent.\n5. **GraphQL `resolveReviewThread` shape is unknown.** Zero repo precedent; route the\n   `databaseId`-pagination + mutation shape capture into the §19.1 probe (alongside\n   `in_reply_to_id` and the Augment bot login).\n6. **`loop_guard`/`run_log`/`recovery` are V1 vapor.** They are named in `pr_submit/__init__.py`\n   but unbuilt. V2's §9 two-phase ledger should be where this durable-state core is finally\n   built — don't assume V1 left a reusable run-log.\n7. **Sandbox tech (OD-1) is greenfield.** No container/microVM execution harness exists; the\n   Docker refs are MCP-gateway-only. The §6 boundary is well-specified but the implementation\n   has no scaffold to start from — likely the single biggest net-new ops lift.\n\n## Stale Documentation Found\n\n- **Spec §9 (swarm:2269 citation)** — **[CODE-CONTRADICTED]** The cited line is a CLI watch-loop\n  `iterations += 1` cap (in-memory, non-durable), not the \"monotonic, disk-authoritative,\n  survives restarts\" counter the prose claims. The described primitive does not exist at that\n  citation.\n- **Spec Reuse Map / §20** — **[CODE-CONTRADICTED in spirit]** §20 calls V1.0 \"fully replaced\"\n  and the Reuse Map credits no V1 code, but `src/superclaude/pr_submit/` is a built, tested\n  package whose FSM/router/detection/models are 40-80% applicable to V2's \"New\" H2/D5/§19.1\n  components. \"Fully replaced\" overstates the discontinuity at the code level.\n- **`pr_submit/__init__.py` docstring** — **[CODE-CONTRADICTED]** lists `loop_guard`, `run_log`,\n  `recovery` as package modules; those files do not exist (the docstring hedges \"wired\n  incrementally as the modules land,\" but a reader scanning the docstring would assume they're\n  present).
